SPARK 3-Player Team Backdrops

Grok prompts for the 3v1 team backdrops. The solo player always sits top-left (north-west, where player one is). The team of three holds the other three quarters - top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left - as one continuous L with no walls. Each image is the whole board, wide 16:9, and its top-left quarter must stay plain because the solo player's own art covers it. Recommended: 56 images, one per race trio, with the seat rule below. None are needed for v1 - teammates keep their own quadrant art until these exist.

NEW (S196): the 12 single-race prompts - each race's 4-player quadrant and 2-player half, in this same Grok style - are at the bottom of this page.

The layout - the important technicality

One continuous scene runs through the blue L - top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left - and blends softly across the two dotted seams. The hatched top-left quarter is x 0-50%, y 0-50% of the image (x 0-584, y 0-392 px at 1168 x 784): plain, dark, low-detail ground with nothing in it. Every prompt names which race goes in NE, SE and SW and repeats this rule. The centre disc (the quarry) and the three outer corners (castles) stay plain too.

Technical spec

Layout (owner R195-T2)
3v1: the solo player is ALWAYS top-left (NW, where player one sits). The team of three holds NE + SE + SW as one continuous L with no walls between them. SE is the elbow: it borders both teammates; NE and SW touch each other only at the quarry.
Region one image covers
The WHOLE board, 1920 x 1080 world px (16:9, constants.ts:95-96). Quarters are 960 x 540 each, clock order 0 = NW, 1 = NE, 2 = SE, 3 = SW (zoneBackgroundRenderer.ts:308-320). The NW quarter (x 0-960, y 0-540) is covered in game by the solo player's own race art.
What to ask Grok for
Landscape 16:9 on grok.com (Grok Imagine). Expect about 1168 x 784 back - the landscape counterpart of your Grok 2v2 files, which measured 784 x 1168 (an estimate, not measured on a landscape request). Any wide, 16:9-ish size works: the processing cover-crops it. Never portrait, never square.
The NW quarter, exactly
x 0-50%, y 0-50% of the frame = x 0-584, y 0-392 px at 1168 x 784 (= x 0-960, y 0-540 on the board). If Grok returns another size it is always the top-left W/2 x H/2.
Crop + resize (wiring tree)
Cover-scale to 1920 x 1080, centre-crop (1168 x 784 loses ~8% top and bottom; a true 16:9 loses ~0), LANCZOS-resize to 960 x 540 PNG RGB - half resolution, the same rule as every shipped zone image (the S166 texture-memory fix).
Keep plain (measured)
Centre: quarry disc r = 127 at (960,540) is punched out (about a quarter of the frame height across). Outer corners: castles at (1790,130), (1790,950), (130,950). Bottom 84 px: footer bar (y 996-1080, 7.8%).
Brightness gate
Shipped zone art: mean max-channel 22-41 / 255, zero px > 200. Your 2v2 set measured 27-49. Check below.
Where files go
You: download full size into C:\Users\onesh\Downloads (same as the 2v2 set) as Team3_<NE>_<SE>_<SW>.jpg, e.g. Team3_Demons_Mummies_Nagas.jpg. Shipped (later, wiring tree): public/art/race-zones/zone-team3-<ne>-<se>-<sw>.png.

How many images - 56 recommended

The count

  • Any race in any of NE / SE / SW: 6 x 6 x 6 = 216.
  • Free choice of which race takes the elbow (SE): 6 + 30 x 2 + 20 x 3 = 126.
  • Recommended: the game seats a trio by a fixed rule, so one image per race trio = C(8,3) = 56: 20 with three different races, 30 with a pair plus one, 6 all one race.
  • No flip trick cuts this further: swapping NE and SW would need a transpose, which a wide frame cannot survive.

The seat rule (recommendation - your call)

Sort the three teammates by the fixed race order Demons < Mummies < Nagas < Orcs < Vampires < Zombies (alphabetical, the same order the 2v2 file names use). The first goes NE (top-right), the second SE (bottom-right, the elbow), the third SW (bottom-left). Teammates of the same race are interchangeable.

Trade-off to know: NE and SW each border the solo player, SE borders only teammates. With this rule the middle race in the order always gets the sheltered elbow. If that matters, it is 126 images (you pick the elbow) or 216.

Chips read NE / SE / SW. Green = done (saved in this browser).

How to generate in Grok

Steps

  1. Open grok.com, start a new chat for every image and use Grok's image generation (Grok Imagine) - the tool your whole 2v2 set was made on (R194-19). Ask for a wide 16:9 landscape.
  2. Recommended: attach (upload) to the Grok chat the two 2v2 images listed on the card (your own finished Grok <A>X<B>.jpg files) and add: "Use the attached images ONLY for palette, materials, level of detail and camera - not for their layout or framing."
  3. Paste the prompt. If the result is not wide: reply "Same image, but as a wide landscape 16:9 frame." If the top-left has anything in it: "Same image, but make the top-left quarter plain, dark, empty ground with nothing in it."
  4. 2-4 tries per trio, keep the best. Download at full size (download button, not a screenshot) and save with the card's file name.
  5. Tick the card. When a batch is done, send the folder path to a session; it crops, resizes, runs the checks and wires.

Reject if

  • Anything in the top-left quarter: a ruin, a glow, a landmark, a character, or a visible box/edge around it.
  • A wall, road, river, fence or straight line between the three team quarters, or a three-panel / split-screen look.
  • A race in the wrong quarter (check the card: NE, SE, SW).
  • Sky, horizon, moon or a side-on view; portrait or square output.
  • Bright or white patches, neon colour, a busy high-contrast picture; text, rune-letters, a watermark, a frame or a vignette.
  • Landmarks at the dead centre, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip.
  • Anything that looks like a known game, film or franchise.

#01

NE Demons · SE Demons · SW Demons

same race
Top-left stays empty (solo opponent). Top-right Demons, bottom-right Demons, bottom-left Demons.
Save as Team3_Demons_Demons_Demons.jpg · style refs: C:\Users\onesh\Downloads\DemonsXDemons.jpg
One continuous battlefield ground covering a WHOLE game board, seen from high above: three allied worlds that flow into each other in an L-SHAPE around one quiet, empty quarter. Picture the frame cut into four equal quarters by an invisible cross through its exact centre (never draw the cross). WHO GOES WHERE: TOP-RIGHT (north-east) = DEMONS, BOTTOM-RIGHT (south-east) = DEMONS, BOTTOM-LEFT (south-west) = DEMONS, TOP-LEFT (north-west) = nobody. THE TOP-LEFT QUARTER IS NOT PART OF THIS SCENE - EXACT: the quarter from the left edge to the middle and from the top edge to the middle (x 0-50%, y 0-50% of the frame; in a 1168 x 784 frame that is x 0-584 px, y 0-392 px). In the game it is completely covered by a different picture. Fill it only with plain, dark, low-detail ground that quietly continues the colours of the two quarters beside it and fades toward near-black: no landmarks, no ruins, no structures, no glow, no focal point, nothing worth looking at. Do not draw a box, an edge, a wall or a border around it, and do not leave it flat solid black - it is soft, neutral ground that could be covered without anything being lost. Every landmark, ruin and glow in the image belongs in the other three quarters. TOP-RIGHT, BOTTOM-RIGHT AND BOTTOM-LEFT QUARTERS - all three are DEMONS ground, one world for one team of three: an infernal wasteland: cracked black basalt and cooling lava fissures, jagged obsidian shards, the ruined ribs of a bone-and-iron arch lying collapsed flat on the ground, ash drifting slowly, thin black smoke. Everything is deep charcoal and volcanic near-black. Faint MAGENTA accents only: a violet-magenta glow deep inside the fissures and a magenta cast in the drifting smoke. Give each of the three quarters its own landmarks so the L never looks copied, tiled or mirrored. THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left is ONE unbroken piece of the same ground shared by three allies - no seams, no dividing lines, no walls, no fences, no roads or river edges between the quarters. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; ANY landmark, ruin, structure, glow or detail in the TOP-LEFT QUARTER; a box, edge, frame, wall or border drawn around the top-left quarter; a straight dividing line, a wall, a fence, a road or a river between the quarters, a split-screen, three-panel, four-panel or grid look, mirrored or symmetrical quarters; anything at the dead centre of the frame, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip; portrait or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

BOTTOM-LEFT QUARTER (south-west) - ORCS: a badlands war-camp at dusk: cracked red-brown hardpan and scattered bones, the pointed stake-tips of a crude timber palisade, round hide-tent roofs, the plank platform of a rough watchtower, the broken rim of a mesa cliff, dust hanging low. Everything is deep rust-black and dull brown. Faint BURNT-ORANGE accents only: two or three low banked cook-fires glowing dully behind the palisade and a thin orange glow in the dust. THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the three worlds form ONE piece of ground shared by three allies, an L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left. Where the top-right meets the bottom-right (along the right half of the horizontal middle): both quarters are DEMONS ground - one unbroken stretch of the same world with no seam at all, varied enough that it never looks repeated, tiled or mirrored. Where the bottom-right meets the bottom-left (along the bottom half of the vertical middle): the basalt fissures fade out and the ground turns into scorched, cracked red-brown hardpan, with ash settling over the dust. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; ANY landmark, ruin, structure, glow or detail in the TOP-LEFT QUARTER; a box, edge, frame, wall or border drawn around the top-left quarter; a straight dividing line, a wall, a fence, a road or a river between the quarters, a split-screen, three-panel, four-panel or grid look, mirrored or symmetrical quarters; anything at the dead centre of the frame, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip; portrait or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

In the game it is completely covered by a different picture. Fill it only with plain, dark, low-detail ground that quietly continues the colours of the two quarters beside it and fades toward near-black: no landmarks, no ruins, no structures, no glow, no focal point, nothing worth looking at. Do not draw a box, an edge, a wall or a border around it, and do not leave it flat solid black - it is soft, neutral ground that could be covered without anything being lost. Every landmark, ruin and glow in the image belongs in the other three quarters. TOP-RIGHT QUARTER (north-east) - DEMONS: an infernal wasteland: cracked black basalt and cooling lava fissures, jagged obsidian shards, the ruined ribs of a bone-and-iron arch lying collapsed flat on the ground, ash drifting slowly, thin black smoke. Everything is deep charcoal and volcanic near-black. Faint MAGENTA accents only: a violet-magenta glow deep inside the fissures and a magenta cast in the drifting smoke. BOTTOM-RIGHT QUARTER (south-east) - MUMMIES: a moonlit desert necropolis: low wind-rippled dunes and half-buried sandstone blocks, the stepped square summit of a weathered step-pyramid, the toppled fragments of a colossal broken statue lying in the sand, wind-blown sand hazing the air. Everything is deep umber-black and cold grey-brown. Faint AMBER-GOLD accents only: a dull gold glimmer on one carved stone lintel and a warm amber haze low over the sand. BOTTOM-LEFT QUARTER (south-west) - ORCS: a badlands war-camp at dusk: cracked red-brown hardpan and scattered bones, the pointed stake-tips of a crude timber palisade, round hide-tent roofs, the plank platform of a rough watchtower, the broken rim of a mesa cliff, dust hanging low. Everything is deep rust-black and dull brown. Faint BURNT-ORANGE accents only: two or three low banked cook-fires glowing dully behind the palisade and a thin orange glow in the dust. THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the three worlds form ONE piece of ground shared by three allies, an L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left. Where the top-right meets the bottom-right (along the right half of the horizontal middle): the black basalt cools and crumbles into ash-dusted dark sand, with a few glassy, heat-fused patches where the two grounds meet. Where the bottom-right meets the bottom-left (along the bottom half of the vertical middle): the dunes flatten out into cracked red-brown hardpan; sand drifts in thin tongues across the dry clay and scattered bones. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. BOTTOM-RIGHT QUARTER (south-east) - NAGAS: a drowned military citadel on the deep sea floor: toppled basalt columns lying on the silt, the ribbed hull of a sunken war-galley, the harsh angular foundations of fortress walls, a broken sea-god statue lying face-down, silt drifting in slow currents, the water almost black. Everything is deep teal-black and slate. Faint CYAN accents only: a cold cyan glow leaking from arrow-slits in the sunken wall tops and a thin cyan shimmer on the drifting silt. BOTTOM-LEFT QUARTER (south-west) - ORCS: a badlands war-camp at dusk: cracked red-brown hardpan and scattered bones, the pointed stake-tips of a crude timber palisade, round hide-tent roofs, the plank platform of a rough watchtower, the broken rim of a mesa cliff, dust hanging low. Everything is deep rust-black and dull brown. Faint BURNT-ORANGE accents only: two or three low banked cook-fires glowing dully behind the palisade and a thin orange glow in the dust. THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the three worlds form ONE piece of ground shared by three allies, an L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left. Where the top-right meets the bottom-right (along the right half of the horizontal middle): the black basalt slopes down into shallow black water; where the last fissures meet the sea a faint low steam haze hangs and the rock cools into dark rounded pillow-stone. Where the bottom-right meets the bottom-left (along the bottom half of the vertical middle): the sea floor shelves up into a dark muddy tidal flat that dries out into cracked red-brown hardpan. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; ANY landmark, ruin, structure, glow or detail in the TOP-LEFT QUARTER; a box, edge, frame, wall or border drawn around the top-left quarter; a straight dividing line, a wall, a fence, a road or a river between the quarters, a split-screen, three-panel, four-panel or grid look, mirrored or symmetrical quarters; anything at the dead centre of the frame, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip; portrait or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. One continuous battlefield ground covering a WHOLE game board, seen from high above: three allied worlds that flow into each other in an L-SHAPE around one quiet, empty quarter. Picture the frame cut into four equal quarters by an invisible cross through its exact centre (never draw the cross). WHO GOES WHERE: TOP-RIGHT (north-east) = DEMONS, BOTTOM-RIGHT (south-east) = ORCS, BOTTOM-LEFT (south-west) = ZOMBIES, TOP-LEFT (north-west) = nobody. THE TOP-LEFT QUARTER IS NOT PART OF THIS SCENE - EXACT: the quarter from the left edge to the middle and from the top edge to the middle (x 0-50%, y 0-50% of the frame; in a 1168 x 784 frame that is x 0-584 px, y 0-392 px). In the game it is completely covered by a different picture. Fill it only with plain, dark, low-detail ground that quietly continues the colours of the two quarters beside it and fades toward near-black: no landmarks, no ruins, no structures, no glow, no focal point, nothing worth looking at. Do not draw a box, an edge, a wall or a border around it, and do not leave it flat solid black - it is soft, neutral ground that could be covered without anything being lost. Every landmark, ruin and glow in the image belongs in the other three quarters. TOP-RIGHT QUARTER (north-east) - DEMONS: an infernal wasteland: cracked black basalt and cooling lava fissures, jagged obsidian shards, the ruined ribs of a bone-and-iron arch lying collapsed flat on the ground, ash drifting slowly, thin black smoke. Everything is deep charcoal and volcanic near-black. Faint MAGENTA accents only: a violet-magenta glow deep inside the fissures and a magenta cast in the drifting smoke. BOTTOM-RIGHT QUARTER (south-east) - ORCS: a badlands war-camp at dusk: cracked red-brown hardpan and scattered bones, the pointed stake-tips of a crude timber palisade, round hide-tent roofs, the plank platform of a rough watchtower, the broken rim of a mesa cliff, dust hanging low. Everything is deep rust-black and dull brown. Faint BURNT-ORANGE accents only: two or three low banked cook-fires glowing dully behind the palisade and a thin orange glow in the dust. BOTTOM-LEFT QUARTER (south-west) - ZOMBIES: a rotting swamp of ruined cottages: black still water and mats of dead reeds, the broken shingle roofs of collapsed timber cottages, flat gravestone slabs half-sunk in mud, the bare branching silhouettes of dead trees, a low clinging fog lying over the water. Everything is deep bog-green-black and wet grey. Faint SICKLY GREEN accents only: a dull swamp-glow rising off the water in two or three patches and a faint green phosphorescence on the rotten timber. THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the three worlds form ONE piece of ground shared by three allies, an L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left. Where the top-right meets the bottom-right (along the right half of the horizontal middle): the basalt fissures fade out and the ground turns into scorched, cracked red-brown hardpan, with ash settling over the dust. Where the bottom-right meets the bottom-left (along the bottom half of the vertical middle): the cracked hardpan softens into wet mud and stagnant black pools; the hanging dust gives way to low fog. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. Every landmark, ruin and glow in the image belongs in the other three quarters. TOP-RIGHT, BOTTOM-RIGHT AND BOTTOM-LEFT QUARTERS - all three are MUMMIES ground, one world for one team of three: a moonlit desert necropolis: low wind-rippled dunes and half-buried sandstone blocks, the stepped square summit of a weathered step-pyramid, the toppled fragments of a colossal broken statue lying in the sand, wind-blown sand hazing the air. THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left is ONE unbroken piece of the same ground shared by three allies - no seams, no dividing lines, no walls, no fences, no roads or river edges between the quarters. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; ANY landmark, ruin, structure, glow or detail in the TOP-LEFT QUARTER; a box, edge, frame, wall or border drawn around the top-left quarter; a straight dividing line, a wall, a fence, a road or a river between the quarters, a split-screen, three-panel, four-panel or grid look, mirrored or symmetrical quarters; anything at the dead centre of the frame, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip; portrait or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Every landmark, ruin and glow in the image belongs in the other three quarters. TOP-RIGHT QUARTER (north-east) - MUMMIES: a moonlit desert necropolis: low wind-rippled dunes and half-buried sandstone blocks, the stepped square summit of a weathered step-pyramid, the toppled fragments of a colossal broken statue lying in the sand, wind-blown sand hazing the air. Everything is deep umber-black and cold grey-brown. Faint AMBER-GOLD accents only: a dull gold glimmer on one carved stone lintel and a warm amber haze low over the sand. BOTTOM-RIGHT QUARTER (south-east) - NAGAS: a drowned military citadel on the deep sea floor: toppled basalt columns lying on the silt, the ribbed hull of a sunken war-galley, the harsh angular foundations of fortress walls, a broken sea-god statue lying face-down, silt drifting in slow currents, the water almost black. Picture the frame cut into four equal quarters by an invisible cross through its exact centre (never draw the cross). WHO GOES WHERE: TOP-RIGHT (north-east) = MUMMIES, BOTTOM-RIGHT (south-east) = ORCS, BOTTOM-LEFT (south-west) = ORCS, TOP-LEFT (north-west) = nobody. THE TOP-LEFT QUARTER IS NOT PART OF THIS SCENE - EXACT: the quarter from the left edge to the middle and from the top edge to the middle (x 0-50%, y 0-50% of the frame; in a 1168 x 784 frame that is x 0-584 px, y 0-392 px). In the game it is completely covered by a different picture. Fill it only with plain, dark, low-detail ground that quietly continues the colours of the two quarters beside it and fades toward near-black: no landmarks, no ruins, no structures, no glow, no focal point, nothing worth looking at. Do not draw a box, an edge, a wall or a border around it, and do not leave it flat solid black - it is soft, neutral ground that could be covered without anything being lost. Every landmark, ruin and glow in the image belongs in the other three quarters. TOP-RIGHT QUARTER (north-east) - MUMMIES: a moonlit desert necropolis: low wind-rippled dunes and half-buried sandstone blocks, the stepped square summit of a weathered step-pyramid, the toppled fragments of a colossal broken statue lying in the sand, wind-blown sand hazing the air. Everything is deep umber-black and cold grey-brown. Faint AMBER-GOLD accents only: a dull gold glimmer on one carved stone lintel and a warm amber haze low over the sand. BOTTOM-RIGHT QUARTER (south-east) - ORCS: a badlands war-camp at dusk: cracked red-brown hardpan and scattered bones, the pointed stake-tips of a crude timber palisade, round hide-tent roofs, the plank platform of a rough watchtower, the broken rim of a mesa cliff, dust hanging low. Everything is deep rust-black and dull brown. Faint BURNT-ORANGE accents only: two or three low banked cook-fires glowing dully behind the palisade and a thin orange glow in the dust. BOTTOM-LEFT QUARTER (south-west) - ORCS: a badlands war-camp at dusk: cracked red-brown hardpan and scattered bones, the pointed stake-tips of a crude timber palisade, round hide-tent roofs, the plank platform of a rough watchtower, the broken rim of a mesa cliff, dust hanging low. Everything is deep rust-black and dull brown. Faint BURNT-ORANGE accents only: two or three low banked cook-fires glowing dully behind the palisade and a thin orange glow in the dust. THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the three worlds form ONE piece of ground shared by three allies, an L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left. Where the top-right meets the bottom-right (along the right half of the horizontal middle): the dunes flatten out into cracked red-brown hardpan; sand drifts in thin tongues across the dry clay and scattered bones. Where the bottom-right meets the bottom-left (along the bottom half of the vertical middle): both quarters are ORCS ground - one unbroken stretch of the same world with no seam at all, varied enough that it never looks repeated, tiled or mirrored. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; ANY landmark, ruin, structure, glow or detail in the TOP-LEFT QUARTER; a box, edge, frame, wall or border drawn around the top-left quarter; a straight dividing line, a wall, a fence, a road or a river between the quarters, a split-screen, three-panel, four-panel or grid look, mirrored or symmetrical quarters; anything at the dead centre of the frame, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip; portrait or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

Where the top-right meets the bottom-right (along the right half of the horizontal middle): the dunes give way to rocky scree and then to the first black pine treetops; cold mist lies low over the last of the sand. Where the bottom-right meets the bottom-left (along the bottom half of the vertical middle): the pine forest drops into a sodden hollow where the trees die and the ground turns to black swamp water; the mist and the swamp fog merge into one. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; ANY landmark, ruin, structure, glow or detail in the TOP-LEFT QUARTER; a box, edge, frame, wall or border drawn around the top-left quarter; a straight dividing line, a wall, a fence, a road or a river between the quarters, a split-screen, three-panel, four-panel or grid look, mirrored or symmetrical quarters; anything at the dead centre of the frame, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip; portrait or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

One continuous battlefield ground covering a WHOLE game board, seen from high above: three allied worlds that flow into each other in an L-SHAPE around one quiet, empty quarter. Picture the frame cut into four equal quarters by an invisible cross through its exact centre (never draw the cross). WHO GOES WHERE: TOP-RIGHT (north-east) = NAGAS, BOTTOM-RIGHT (south-east) = NAGAS, BOTTOM-LEFT (south-west) = ZOMBIES, TOP-LEFT (north-west) = nobody. THE TOP-LEFT QUARTER IS NOT PART OF THIS SCENE - EXACT: the quarter from the left edge to the middle and from the top edge to the middle (x 0-50%, y 0-50% of the frame; in a 1168 x 784 frame that is x 0-584 px, y 0-392 px). In the game it is completely covered by a different picture. Fill it only with plain, dark, low-detail ground that quietly continues the colours of the two quarters beside it and fades toward near-black: no landmarks, no ruins, no structures, no glow, no focal point, nothing worth looking at. Do not draw a box, an edge, a wall or a border around it, and do not leave it flat solid black - it is soft, neutral ground that could be covered without anything being lost. Every landmark, ruin and glow in the image belongs in the other three quarters. TOP-RIGHT QUARTER (north-east) - NAGAS: a drowned military citadel on the deep sea floor: toppled basalt columns lying on the silt, the ribbed hull of a sunken war-galley, the harsh angular foundations of fortress walls, a broken sea-god statue lying face-down, silt drifting in slow currents, the water almost black. Everything is deep teal-black and slate. Faint CYAN accents only: a cold cyan glow leaking from arrow-slits in the sunken wall tops and a thin cyan shimmer on the drifting silt. BOTTOM-RIGHT QUARTER (south-east) - NAGAS: a drowned military citadel on the deep sea floor: toppled basalt columns lying on the silt, the ribbed hull of a sunken war-galley, the harsh angular foundations of fortress walls, a broken sea-god statue lying face-down, silt drifting in slow currents, the water almost black. Everything is deep teal-black and slate. Faint CYAN accents only: a cold cyan glow leaking from arrow-slits in the sunken wall tops and a thin cyan shimmer on the drifting silt. BOTTOM-LEFT QUARTER (south-west) - ZOMBIES: a rotting swamp of ruined cottages: black still water and mats of dead reeds, the broken shingle roofs of collapsed timber cottages, flat gravestone slabs half-sunk in mud, the bare branching silhouettes of dead trees, a low clinging fog lying over the water. Everything is deep bog-green-black and wet grey. Faint SICKLY GREEN accents only: a dull swamp-glow rising off the water in two or three patches and a faint green phosphorescence on the rotten timber. THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the three worlds form ONE piece of ground shared by three allies, an L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left. Where the top-right meets the bottom-right (along the right half of the horizontal middle): both quarters are NAGAS ground - one unbroken stretch of the same world with no seam at all, varied enough that it never looks repeated, tiled or mirrored. Where the bottom-right meets the bottom-left (along the bottom half of the vertical middle): the deep sea floor shallows into black brackish swamp water; drowned reeds and drifting silt mix together. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; ANY landmark, ruin, structure, glow or detail in the TOP-LEFT QUARTER; a box, edge, frame, wall or border drawn around the top-left quarter; a straight dividing line, a wall, a fence, a road or a river between the quarters, a split-screen, three-panel, four-panel or grid look, mirrored or symmetrical quarters; anything at the dead centre of the frame, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip; portrait or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the three worlds form ONE piece of ground shared by three allies, an L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left. Where the top-right meets the bottom-right (along the right half of the horizontal middle): the sea floor rises into the wet rock of a black mountain-lake shore; dark pine treetops crowd the waterline under the mist. Where the bottom-right meets the bottom-left (along the bottom half of the vertical middle): both quarters are VAMPIRES ground - one unbroken stretch of the same world with no seam at all, varied enough that it never looks repeated, tiled or mirrored. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; ANY landmark, ruin, structure, glow or detail in the TOP-LEFT QUARTER; a box, edge, frame, wall or border drawn around the top-left quarter; a straight dividing line, a wall, a fence, a road or a river between the quarters, a split-screen, three-panel, four-panel or grid look, mirrored or symmetrical quarters; anything at the dead centre of the frame, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip; portrait or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

TOP-RIGHT QUARTER (north-east) - NAGAS: a drowned military citadel on the deep sea floor: toppled basalt columns lying on the silt, the ribbed hull of a sunken war-galley, the harsh angular foundations of fortress walls, a broken sea-god statue lying face-down, silt drifting in slow currents, the water almost black. Everything is deep teal-black and slate. Faint CYAN accents only: a cold cyan glow leaking from arrow-slits in the sunken wall tops and a thin cyan shimmer on the drifting silt. Everything is deep bog-green-black and wet grey. One continuous battlefield ground covering a WHOLE game board, seen from high above: three allied worlds that flow into each other in an L-SHAPE around one quiet, empty quarter. Picture the frame cut into four equal quarters by an invisible cross through its exact centre (never draw the cross). WHO GOES WHERE: TOP-RIGHT (north-east) = ORCS, BOTTOM-RIGHT (south-east) = ORCS, BOTTOM-LEFT (south-west) = ZOMBIES, TOP-LEFT (north-west) = nobody. THE TOP-LEFT QUARTER IS NOT PART OF THIS SCENE - EXACT: the quarter from the left edge to the middle and from the top edge to the middle (x 0-50%, y 0-50% of the frame; in a 1168 x 784 frame that is x 0-584 px, y 0-392 px). In the game it is completely covered by a different picture. Fill it only with plain, dark, low-detail ground that quietly continues the colours of the two quarters beside it and fades toward near-black: no landmarks, no ruins, no structures, no glow, no focal point, nothing worth looking at. Do not draw a box, an edge, a wall or a border around it, and do not leave it flat solid black - it is soft, neutral ground that could be covered without anything being lost. Every landmark, ruin and glow in the image belongs in the other three quarters. TOP-RIGHT QUARTER (north-east) - ORCS: a badlands war-camp at dusk: cracked red-brown hardpan and scattered bones, the pointed stake-tips of a crude timber palisade, round hide-tent roofs, the plank platform of a rough watchtower, the broken rim of a mesa cliff, dust hanging low. Everything is deep rust-black and dull brown. Faint BURNT-ORANGE accents only: two or three low banked cook-fires glowing dully behind the palisade and a thin orange glow in the dust. BOTTOM-RIGHT QUARTER (south-east) - ORCS: a badlands war-camp at dusk: cracked red-brown hardpan and scattered bones, the pointed stake-tips of a crude timber palisade, round hide-tent roofs, the plank platform of a rough watchtower, the broken rim of a mesa cliff, dust hanging low. Everything is deep rust-black and dull brown. Faint BURNT-ORANGE accents only: two or three low banked cook-fires glowing dully behind the palisade and a thin orange glow in the dust. BOTTOM-LEFT QUARTER (south-west) - ZOMBIES: a rotting swamp of ruined cottages: black still water and mats of dead reeds, the broken shingle roofs of collapsed timber cottages, flat gravestone slabs half-sunk in mud, the bare branching silhouettes of dead trees, a low clinging fog lying over the water. Everything is deep bog-green-black and wet grey. Faint SICKLY GREEN accents only: a dull swamp-glow rising off the water in two or three patches and a faint green phosphorescence on the rotten timber. THE SEAMS - ONE CONTINUOUS ZONE, NO WALLS: the three worlds form ONE piece of ground shared by three allies, an L that runs from the top-right down to the bottom-right and across to the bottom-left. Where the top-right meets the bottom-right (along the right half of the horizontal middle): both quarters are ORCS ground - one unbroken stretch of the same world with no seam at all, varied enough that it never looks repeated, tiled or mirrored. Where the bottom-right meets the bottom-left (along the bottom half of the vertical middle): the cracked hardpan softens into wet mud and stagnant black pools; the hanging dust gives way to low fog. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a WIDE LANDSCAPE image, aspect ratio 16:9 (a wide frame such as 1168 x 784 is fine) - the shape of the whole game board, never portrait, never square. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is no sky, no horizon, no moon, no side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. LAYOUT: the exact centre of the frame is a shared spawn portal that the game cuts out - keep a round area about a quarter of the frame height across, at the dead centre, plain and dark. The three outer corners (top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), roughly an eighth of the frame width in from the side edge and a fifth of the frame height in from the top or bottom edge, are where castles stand - keep them plain and dark too. The bottom 8% of the frame lies under a toolbar - keep it plain. Put each world's two or three landmarks in the middle of its own quarter, between the centre and its corner. Nothing important touches the top-left quarter. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; ANY landmark, ruin, structure, glow or detail in the TOP-LEFT QUARTER; a box, edge, frame, wall or border drawn around the top-left quarter; a straight dividing line, a wall, a fence, a road or a river between the quarters, a split-screen, three-panel, four-panel or grid look, mirrored or symmetrical quarters; anything at the dead centre of the frame, in the three outer corners or in the bottom strip; portrait or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

TOP-RIGHT QUARTER (north-east) - VAMPIRES: a mountain valley at night: black jagged pine forest seen as dense dark treetops, steep bare crag ledges of grey rock, a thin cold mist lying in the hollows, the slate roofs of a few distant stone houses. Everything is deep blue-black and charcoal. Faint CRIMSON accents only: a dull red glow in a handful of small roof-windows and a cold crimson tint along the edge of the mist. BOTTOM-RIGHT QUARTER (south-east) - VAMPIRES: a mountain valley at night: black jagged pine forest seen as dense dark treetops, steep bare crag ledges of grey rock, a thin cold mist lying in the hollows, the slate roofs of a few distant stone houses. Each blend is gradual and irregular, roughly a tenth of the frame wide; never a straight line, never a wall, a fence, a road, a river edge or a cliff line, never a split-screen or a three-panel look. Each world stays clearly recognisable in the middle of its own quarter. S196 - the 12 single-race images (6 races x 4-player quadrant + 2-player half)

The same Grok style, camera and dark palette as your pair and trio images, one world per picture. Each race needs two: a wide one for the 4-player board and a tall one for the 1v1 board.

Why
Owner R196-A1/A2: your Grok images are far better than the original Gemini single-race art, so the six races get the same top-down Grok look in both single-race shapes. These replace today's zone-<race>-4p.png / zone-<race>-2p.png.
4-player quadrant
Fills one quarter of the 4-player board: 960 x 540 world px, 16:9. Used in every seat's quarter, never flipped: castle in the OUTER corner (any of the four), the portal in the INNER corner. Also used for a 1v1v2 solo and, until your trio image exists, each 3v1 teammate. Shipped at 480 x 270.
2-player half
Fills one half of the 1v1 board: 960 x 1080 world px, 8:9, left or right, never flipped: castle at the middle of the outer side edge, the portal at the middle of the inner one. Also the 2v1 solo's whole side (castle top-left). Shipped at 480 x 540.
What to ask Grok for
4-player: landscape 16:9 (expect ~1168 x 784, as your trio files). 2-player: portrait 2:3 (expect ~784 x 1168, as your pair files). The session cover-crops: the 4-player loses ~8% top and bottom, the 2-player ~12%.
Where files go
Download full size into C:\Users\onesh\Downloads as Single4P_<Race>.jpg and Single2P_<Race>.jpg, e.g. Single4P_Demons.jpg. A session transcodes and swaps them in.

Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. So: CORNERS - all four corners of the picture are the quietest, plainest, darkest ground in it, because the player's castle stands in the OUTER corner (any of the four, depending on the seat) and the shared spawn portal sits in the INNER corner (the opposite one). EDGES: the outer band of the picture on every side (about the outer eighth of the width and of the height) is calm, low-detail ground that gets slowly darker and flatter toward the edge - next to an enemy there is a wall line, next to a teammate the game fades this world softly into his, so nothing may touch or run along an edge: no landmark, ruin, road, river, wall, fence, shoreline or tree line at an edge. The edges must not look like a frame either: no border, no vignette ring - just ground that calmly runs out of detail. CENTRE: put the world's two or three landmarks in the middle area of the picture, inside the central half, spread out a little so it does not read as one bullseye. CAMERA AND FRAME - EXACT: a TALL PORTRAIT image, aspect ratio 2:3 (a tall frame such as 784 x 1168 is fine). It will be centre-cropped to 8:9, almost square, so keep anything important out of the top and bottom 13% of the frame. Seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down: ruins and objects show only a slight three-quarter tilt, and there is NO sky, NO horizon, NO moon, NO side view and no perspective receding into the distance. The only light is the faint glow coming from the ground itself. A DARK, MOODY, LOW-CONTRAST environment backdrop for a game board, with no characters and no foreground clutter. Painted in a stylised cartoon-illustration style with soft ink linework and flat shading, a little more realistic and less saturated than a poster - muted, near-monochrome, atmospheric. IT MUST STAY DARK AND QUIET. Overall value is deep and shadowed, like ground lit only by moonlight or distant embers. NO bright passages, no white or near-white areas, no strong highlights, no hard focal point, no busy detail, nothing that would compete with brightly coloured game pieces drawn on top of it. Think of it as a faded mural seen through darkness. No characters, no people, no creatures, no animals, no vehicles. No text, no lettering, no words, no logo, no border, no frame, no vignette, no UI, no map markers. Fills the entire frame edge to edge with no margin and no letterboxing. An original world, not the look of any existing game, film or franchise. Avoid: sky, horizon, moon, sun, clouds, mountains seen from the side, a side or isometric view, perspective receding into the distance; characters, people, creatures, animals, monsters, skeletons, vehicles, units; standing buildings or castles seen from the side; text, letters, numbers, runes that read as writing, logos, signature, watermark, border, frame, vignette, UI, icons, grid, hex tiles, map markers; bright highlights, white or near-white areas, neon saturation, high contrast, a sharp focal point, busy fine detail; a second race's world or any colour accent other than this world's own; a wall, road, river, fence, shoreline or straight line running along or into an edge of the picture; landmarks in the corners or at the middle of the left or right edge; a symmetrical or mirrored composition; landscape or square framing; the recognisable style, characters or locations of any existing game, film or franchise.

Orcs · 2-player half

tall 2:3 portrait
One race, one world. Replaces public/art/race-zones/zone-orcs-2p.png (480 x 540).
Save as Single2P_Orcs.jpg · style refs: C:\Users\onesh\Downloads\OrcsXOrcs.jpg, C:\Users\onesh\Downloads\Team3_Orcs_Orcs_Orcs.jpg (if you have it)
One HALF of a two-player battlefield, all of it ORCS ground, seen from HIGH ABOVE, almost straight down, like a painted map of the ground - the same camera as a top-down strategy map. THE WORLD: a badlands war-camp at dusk: cracked red-brown hardpan and scattered bones, the pointed stake-tips of a crude timber palisade, round hide-tent roofs, the plank platform of a rough watchtower, the broken rim of a mesa cliff, dust hanging low. Everything is deep rust-black and dull brown. Faint BURNT-ORANGE accents only: two or three low banked cook-fires glowing dully behind the palisade and a thin orange glow in the dust. WHERE IT GOES: in the game this picture fills one whole HALF of the board, the left half or the right half, and the SAME picture is used for both without being flipped. The player's castle stands at the middle of the OUTER side edge and the shared spawn portal sits at the middle of the INNER side edge - so the castle can be at the middle of either the left or the right edge, and the portal at the middle of the other one. In a three-player team game the same picture also covers one player's whole side with his castle in the TOP-LEFT corner. So: QUIET ZONES - a wide plain, dark, low-detail band down the middle of BOTH the left edge and the right edge (most of all at mid-height, about a third of the frame tall), and all four corners, stay empty ground with no landmarks. For the wiring session (not you)
  • The renderer draws every backdrop at 0.55 alpha, so the solo player's NW sprite does NOT hide what is under it. The bake (punchPortal) must erase the team image's NW quarter as well as the quarry, or the team art bleeds through the solo's world.
  • arrangeTeamSeats (src/state/teams.ts:141) never moves seat 0 (the host). In a 3v1 where the host is IN the trio, today's seating cannot put the solo in NW as R195-T2 requires. The wiring tree has to re-seat (or mirror the image - the straight-down camera makes an X/Y flip legal, which moves the empty quarter to any corner; the canonical race order then maps onto the flipped slots).
  • Seats are not equal: NE and SW each border the solo; SE borders only teammates. The race-order rule therefore always gives the alphabetically-middle race the sheltered elbow. If the owner wants seat choice free, see the alternatives under the count.
  • Bake key must include the team composition (the image differs per trio), and the shipped file is 960 x 540 (one texture, ~2.1 MB decoded, vs ~1.6 MB for three 480 x 270 quadrants today).
from PIL import Image
import numpy as np
im = Image.open(SRC).convert('RGB')            # e.g. Downloads/Team3_Demons_Mummies_Nagas.jpg
w, h = 1920, 1080
s = max(w / im.width, h / im.height)
im = im.resize((max(w, round(im.width * s)), max(h, round(im.height * s))), Image.LANCZOS)
l, t = (im.width - w) // 2, (im.height - h) // 2
im = im.crop((l, t, l + w, t + h)).resize((960, 540), Image.LANCZOS)
im.save(DST, optimize=True)                     # public/art/race-zones/zone-team3-<ne>-<se>-<sw>.png
a = np.asarray(im).max(axis=2)
print('mean', a.mean(), 'px>200', (a > 200).sum())          # gate: mean ~22-49, zero px > 200
q = {'NW': a[:270, :480], 'NE': a[:270, 480:], 'SE': a[270:, 480:], 'SW': a[270:, :480]}
print({k: round(float(v.std()), 1) for k, v in q.items()})  # NW should be the calmest (lowest std)
S195 ART tree · worlds and seams verbatim from the S192 2v2 sheet; geometry measured from src/constants.ts, src/state/zones.ts, src/render/zoneBackgroundRenderer.ts, src/state/teams.ts. Plain-text copy: .claude/plans/S195_TEAM3_BACKDROP_PROMPTS.md.
